# Backlog tasks

> Paths and identifiers written before 2026-10-10 may predate the #336 refactor: see [`renames.tsv`](renames.tsv).

One line per open task. Each has a code/doc pointer so a fresh model can
start without a full context load. Source of truth for detail:
[`scope-backlog.md`](scope-backlog.md) (section letters in brackets),
`what-this-proves.md` (what already shipped), `git log`, and the memory
notes under
`.claude/projects/-home-vivo-repo-PORTUALE-portuale/memory/`.

Rules that apply to every task: take expected output from real Portage
(container bed, host `emerge`, or an upstream resolver test), pin it in
`pytests-contract-suite/test_emerge_pretend_contract.py` /
`test_portuale.py`, keep `test_output_invariants.py` green, and run the
full verification pass (`AGENTS.md` step 8). The suite and the container
bed live in the sibling `pmtest` repo since 2026-09-18
(`agent-context.md`, "Where the tests live"); `TEST/...` and `tests/...`
paths in the entries below are the pre-move spelling of what is now
`../pmtest/differential-test-bed/...` and
`../pmtest/pytests-contract-suite/...`. The Python mirror
(`python/emerge_pretend_reference.py`) was removed 2026-09-15 —
[`history/second_python_copy_removal.md`](history/second_python_copy_removal.md); older
entries below that say "dual-language" / "Rust==Python" describe how
they shipped, not a rule for new work.

---

63. **DONE 2026-09-16 — `EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS` is applied as prepended CLI defaults.** Shipped: config resolution moved above argv parsing in `pretend::run` (real's two-pass dance collapses to one — portuale has no `--config-root` CLI; `--help` still returns first), `args_with_emerge_defaults` prepends `shell_split(config.other_vars["EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS"])` unless `--ignore-default-opts`, and both that flag and `--ask-enter-invalid` moved out of the recognized-but-unimplemented table into the parser (the latter was the only host default with no implementation at all; `classify_yes_no`/`ask_confirm`/`ask_select` now reproduce `UserQuery.query`'s `enter_invalid` gate; `ASK_ENTER_INVALID` is reset per `run`). Host check: `--getbinpkg=y` from `/etc/make.local` now yields `[binary gU]` entries. Tests: `pretend::tests::emerge_default_opts_are_prepended_to_argv` (+ the extended `classify_yes_no_*` unit test), contract `test_emerge_default_opts_env_applies_and_argv_wins` / `..._from_a_sourced_make_conf`, help pin updated; `cargo test --release` 1125 passed, pytest 1697 passed. Residue found and filed as #69. Original report follows. **`EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS` is never applied (getbinpkg/usepkg/… ignored).** `resolve_config` stores the variable (`portage-profile` `ENV_SCALAR_VARS` `:1301` → `config.other_vars`), but `pretend::run` (`pretend.rs:7494`) parses argv directly and nothing consumes it, so the host's `EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS="--binpkg-respect-use=y --load-average=11 --autounmask=y --ask-enter-invalid --usepkg=n --getbinpkg=y"` (set in `/etc/make.local`, `source`d by `/etc/portage/make.conf`) is inert. Live: real lists `dev-lang/go`/`dev-build/cmake` as `[binary gU]` + `[f1r] Local copy of remote index…`; portuale lists both as `[ebuild]` (evidence `docs/history/evidence/2026-09-16-world-uDpvN/`). Passing `--getbinpkg` on the command line already works (`portuale emerge -pv --getbinpkg --oneshot dev-build/cmake` → `[binary gU]`), so only the defaults plumbing is missing: real prepends `shlex.split(settings["EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS"])` to argv in a second `parse_opts` pass (`_emerge/main.py:1221-1255,1352-1360`), argv winning. Plan: [`history/01.063-emerge_default_opts.md`](history/01.063-emerge_default_opts.md).

64. **DONE 2026-09-16 — `@world` now includes the `@profile` set.** Shipped: `Config::profile_packages` (unstarred `packages` lines of chain levels whose repo declares `profile-set`, stacked with `stack_mask_lines`; `portage-profile`'s `repo_profile_formats`/`repo_containing` reused), added in real's set order to both `@world` expansions (`pretend.rs` selected/system arms) and to `complete_seed_atoms`; `@profile` is now a first-class target. Committed fixture declares only `portage-2`, so every existing pin stays byte-identical; the positive path is pinned through a copied configroot (`test_profile_set_packages_join_world_and_are_a_target`) plus two `portage-profile` unit tests. Host effect: rclone/linux-firmware/vte/vte-common and weston now resolve, the libdisplay-info→kwin/mesa `rR` cascade disappears, and the `@world` list drops 68→50 with 24 binaries. Original report follows. **`@world` is missing the `@profile` set (rclone/linux-firmware/vte/weston dropped).** Real `@world = @profile @selected @system` (`portage/_sets/__init__.py:97-98`); `@profile` is the **unstarred** `packages` lines of profile levels whose repo declares `profile-set` (`_sets/profiles.py:13-38`), while `@system` keeps the `*`-starred lines of every level. Portuale computes only `system_packages` (`portage-profile/src/lib.rs:3152-3168`) and expands `@world` as selected+system (`pretend.rs:10093-10110`, seed `:10468`), so the host buildovl profile's 100-line `packages` file is dropped: real updates `net-misc/rclone`, `sys-kernel/linux-firmware`, `x11-libs/vte`, `gui-libs/vte-common`; portuale omits them. Missing `dev-libs/weston` also hides its `<media-libs/libdisplay-info-0.4.0:=` bound, letting portuale upgrade libdisplay-info and cascade `rR` onto `kwin`/`kwin-x11`/`mesa`. The committed fixture repo declares only `portage-2` (no `profile-set`), so its pins must stay byte-identical; the host bug is reproduced with a copied configroot. Plan: [`history/01.064-world_profile_set.md`](history/01.064-world_profile_set.md).

67. **DONE 2026-09-16 — USE incremental `-<prefix>_*` wildcard removal missing (VIDEO_CARDS/SANE_BACKENDS `-*`).** S1 `c1447df` added the USE-scoped `apply_use_incremental(_iter)` (dead code + tests), S2 `1065ecc` migrated every USE token-list call site in `portage-profile` and `portage-repo::effective_use_flags_uncached` (the generic/mask/force/keywords folds deliberately keep `apply_incremental`), S3/S4 (this commit) added the `dev-libs/videodrv` fixture + contract pin (copied configroot, real-oracle cells) and the docs. Host effect: xorg-drivers and the three xf86-video-* drivers and sane-backends all leave `@world` (5 packages, nothing added). Original report follows. **USE incremental `-<prefix>_*` wildcard removal missing (VIDEO_CARDS/SANE_BACKENDS `-*`).** The host profile sets `VIDEO_CARDS="-* intel …"` / `SANE_BACKENDS="-* hp …"`; portuale prefixes `-*` to `-video_cards_*` (`prefix_tok` in `portage-profile/src/lib.rs` `resolve_config`) but `apply_incremental_token` only knows literal `-*`, so the clear is a no-op and parent-profile cards plus the ebuild's `+sane_backends_*` IUSE defaults survive. Live (still present post-#63, `docs/history/evidence/2026-09-16-post63-probes/`): portuale reinstalls `x11-base/xorg-drivers` and pulls `xf86-video-{dummy,fbdev,vesa}`, and reinstalls `media-gfx/sane-backends` with every `+sane_backends_*` on; real does neither. Real expands `-*` to `-<var>_*` per profile level (`config.py:2837-2866`), then the USE loop's `x[-2:] == "_*"` arm removes by prefix (`config.py:2925`); the fix must stay USE-scoped (mask/force/keywords folds and generic incrementals have no wildcard arm). Plan reviewed 2026-09-16 (call-site list completed: three `"USE" =>` arms, per-level and conf USE_EXPAND folds, UNPREFIXED, global user USE, `effective_use_flags_uncached`). Slices: S1 easy, S2 medium, S3 medium, S4 trivial. Land **before** #69. Plan: [`history/01.067-use_incremental_prefix_wildcard.md`](history/01.067-use_incremental_prefix_wildcard.md).

68. **DONE 2026-09-16 — false "unsatisfied block" when the blocked installed version is being replaced (host `@world` exits 1).** S1 `9da318f` drops an installed match replaced in its slot (the host shape: `emerge -uDpvN @world` now rc 0, gtk-doc upgraded); **display repair 2026-09-17 (`#68/#72 B0b`–`B5`, branch `backlog/68-74`):** the satisfied `[blocks b      ] <dev-util/gtk-doc-1.36.1 …` row now prints inline right after `gtk-doc-1.36.1` with `Conflict: 1 block (all satisfied)` (B2, the stuck-serializer predicate B0b confirmed), the removal `[uninstall]`/inline `b` rows + `uninst` counter + `--json` entry match real (B3/B4), and the `--columns`/`-q`/`--color` shapes are pinned; host `-uDpvN @world` is 27 identical packages with the same `Conflict:` line. Residues filed: #75 (`--tree`), #76 (`||` wait chains), #77 (nomerge-owner removal rows); evidence `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#68/#72 B0b"–"B4"; S2+S3 `6e2f743` read the owner's operation from its final entry, mark merge-bound matches with a merging parent unresolved, ignore an installed parent's installed matches, apply the soft same-slot skip, and replace the vdb reverse scan with the walked-graph parents test (the 2026-09-16 follow-up below adds the required-set closure after L0 caught the over-narrowing); the renderer gained the teal `b`/red `B` split and the `(all satisfied)` counters suffix. Container oracle cells a–h in `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#68 S0". **S3 follow-up 2026-09-16:** the graph-parents test alone regressed L0's `sys-apps/systemd-utils` (real `B`/rc 1 from installed systemd's `@system` reachability, portuale satisfied); `ResolveCtx::installed_closure` now backs the arm and L0 is back to the pre-existing classes (clean 100 / parity 0.833 / 34 unexplained, `l0-20260916T191018Z`). Residues filed: #72 (`[uninstall]` rows), #73 (set-argument parents). Original report follows. **False "unsatisfied block" when the blocked installed version is being replaced (host `@world` exits 1).** `<dev-util/gtk-doc-1.36.1` from `gtk-doc-am-1.36.1` matches installed `gtk-doc-1.34.0`, but `1.36.1` replaces it in the same run, so real prints `[blocks b]`/`Conflict: 1 block (all satisfied)` and exits 0; portuale's `resolve_blockers` sets `unsolvable = blocked_installed && installed_has_foreign_dependents(...)` with no replaced-in-slot check, so it prints `[blocks B]`/`1 unsatisfied`, the `* Error: … cannot be installed at the same time` block and exits 1 (still present post-#63). **Plan review 2026-09-16 corrected the real model** (`depgraph.py:9095-9255`, read in full): `blocked_final` (package tracker) holds merge-bound matches *and* surviving installed packages; a match is uninstall-ordered when the parent merges and the match is installed, or when the parent is nomerge (uninstall the parent); only a merge-bound match with a merging parent is unresolved outright; an uninstall is abandoned if the installed instance is a digraph node with parents; installed parents' `blocked_initial` matches are ignored; soft same-slot matches are skipped. Portuale gaps: no replaced check, merge-bound matches never unsolvable, same-version reinstall deduplicated into the installed row, `PendingBlocker` has no owner merge/nomerge bit (blockers are also pushed from installed parents in `enqueue_dependencies`), pulled-in test is a vdb reverse scan instead of walked graph entries. Free unsatisfied oracle: `emerge -puDvN --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-containers/runc` → real `[blocks B]`. Slices: S0 oracle cells a–h medium, S1 replaced-in-slot (fixes host rc) medium, S2 merge-bound + `owner_merging` hard, S3 graph-entry pulled-in test medium, S4 pins easy. Plan: [`history/01.068-blocker_replaced_version.md`](history/01.068-blocker_replaced_version.md).

69. **DONE 2026-09-16, repaired 2026-09-17 (`#69 R0`–`R3`, branch `backlog/68-74`) — `--getbinpkg`/`--usepkg` + `--newuse` drops a needed rebuild (found while shipping #63).** Repair (R0–R3, plan [`history/02.68-74.md`](history/02.68-74.md) Phase B): the R0 oracle showed the no-ebuild comparison is reached under `--usepkg` but never under plain `--usepkgonly` (real's respect-use default, `create_depgraph_params.py:47-55`); R1 compares the no-ebuild binary with the profile's selection over its IUSE (real `pkgsettings.setcpv(pkg)`), R2 subtracts force/masked flags (real `flags -= forced_flags`), R3 closes the docs. Bed cells n1–n4 and the host `@world` diff re-verified (`TEST/findings/l0.md` "#69 R0"–"#69 R2"). The 2026-09-16 closure note follows. S0 `ed11de3` staged the `binnew` fixture and captured a real-built binary matrix (12 cells; the crafted fixture `.tbz2`s turned out to be portuale-only, so the oracle pipeline builds one with real `--buildpkgonly` + `--usepkgonly` then adds `newflag` to the ebuild); S1 `33c0479` reworked `binpkg_respect_use_ok` into real's gate (`reinstall_use or (not installed and respect_use)`) with `_reinstall_for_flags`'s arms against the same-version ebuild's IUSE/USE; S2/S3 (this commit) pinned the six cells and closed the docs. Host: `-uDpvN --oneshot dev-qt/qttools` prints `[ebuild R] … LLVM_SLOT="22 -17 … (-23%)"` and qttools is back in `@world`. Original report follows. **`--getbinpkg`/`--usepkg` + `--newuse` drops a needed rebuild (found while shipping #63).** With remote binpkgs eligible (host defaults `--getbinpkg=y --binpkg-respect-use=y`), portuale omits `dev-qt/qttools` from `emerge -uDpvN --oneshot dev-qt/qttools` and from `@world`; real rebuilds it (`[ebuild R] … LLVM_SLOT="22 -17 … (-23%)"`, the ebuild's IUSE gained `llvm_slot_23`). `--usepkg` (local only), `--ignore-default-opts` and `--getbinpkg --usepkg-exclude=dev-qt/qttools` rebuild it; `--binpkg-respect-use=n` does not. **Plan review 2026-09-16 corrected the diagnosis:** real's binary rejection (`depgraph.py:8260-8294`) runs when `reinstall_use` (`--newuse` or any `--reinstall`) **or** respect-use, and compares the binary's baked USE/IUSE against the **same-version ebuild's** profile USE/IUSE (`_reinstall_for_flags`, `:3134-3162`: IUSE symmetric difference counts under `--newuse` or plain respect-use); a rejected binary is skipped and the ebuild is compared with the vdb. `identical_binary` (`:8001-8017`) keeps the *installed* candidate and is not involved (the first plan misread it). Likely root cause: `binpkg_respect_use_ok` runs only under respect-use and evaluates the binary's own IUSE, so an ebuild-only flag is invisible and the binary becomes `best`, after which the newuse diff (binary baked flags vs vdb) is empty. Slices: S0 local-binhost oracle matrix medium, S1 real's rejection gate + ebuild IUSE on both call sites medium, S2 pins + host probes medium, S3 trivial. Land after #67. Plan: [`history/01.069-getbinpkg_newuse_rebuild.md`](history/01.069-getbinpkg_newuse_rebuild.md). Evidence: plan §1 probe table, `docs/history/evidence/2026-09-16-post63-probes/`.

70. **DONE 2026-09-16, repaired 2026-09-16/17 (`#70 R1`–`R5`, branch `backlog/68-74`) — fetch transport overrides are ignored: `FETCHCOMMAND`/`RESUMECOMMAND` (and protocol variants) plus `PORTAGE_RO_DISTDIRS` (found while consolidating #14).** Repair (R1–R5, plan [`history/02.68-74.md`](history/02.68-74.md) Phase A): the S1 parser mangled `\${VAR}`/`\"` (R1), an absent `FETCHCOMMAND` errored instead of defaulting to make.globals (R2), the `${FILE}` refusal was stricter than real (R3), the substitution variables were incomplete (R4); R5 proves a live `ebuild … unpack` download with the default and with a user `FETCHCOMMAND`, verifies the `.mirror-cache.json` round-trips both ways and re-runs L2 (0 unexplained). Shipped: `portage-fetch` gained `FetchCommands` (per-protocol selection, real's `${FILE}` refusal) + `download_with_commands` (POSIX split, `${DISTDIR}`/`${URI}`/`${FILE}` substitution, fresh-failure cleanup) and a public `split_shell_words`; `WgetFetcher`/`Director::fetch` and `portuale::fetch` both take the resolved family (`fetch_commands_from_config` reads the config scalars, `resolved_fetch_commands` resolves the chain in `ebuild_phases`); `FetchOptions::ro_distdirs` (`resolved_ro_distdirs`, existing dirs only) is checked before the fsmirrors copy and every candidate, layout-resolved, digest-checked and symlinked. Unit + integration tests on stub commands and the RO symlink; `FEATURES=distlocks` stays the documented cut. Original report follows. **Fetch transport overrides are ignored: `FETCHCOMMAND`/`RESUMECOMMAND` (and protocol variants) plus `PORTAGE_RO_DISTDIRS` (found while consolidating #14).** `portage_fetch::download_via_wget` hardcodes `make.globals`'s default wget template, so a user's `FETCHCOMMAND="curl …"` (or `FETCHCOMMAND_<PROTO>` and the matching `RESUMECOMMAND*`) is never used; real picks the protocol variant, falls back to the plain name, substitutes `${DISTDIR}`/`${URI}`/`${FILE}` and refuses a command without `${FILE}` (`fetch.py:1652-1700`). Two call sites need it (`portuale/src/fetch.rs` and `mrg-director`'s `Director::fetch`). `PORTAGE_RO_DISTDIRS` (`fetch.py:1055-1059`, `:1456-1475`): existing dirs only, layout-resolved, digest-checked, then **symlinked** into a writable `DISTDIR`; portuale only lists the name in `phase_environ`. Slices: S1 FETCHCOMMAND family medium, S2 RO_DISTDIRS medium, S3 close-out trivial — [`history/01.014-fetch_candidates_mirrors.md`](history/01.014-fetch_candidates_mirrors.md) §5. `FEATURES=distlocks` stays a documented cut.

88. **DONE 2026-09-20 — `bin_dir()`'s per-process PATH overlay is never removed (found auditing pmtest's own /tmp exhaustion, 2026-09-18).** One `libc::atexit` registration where the overlay is created (`ebuild_phases.rs::bin_dir`) removes `$TMPDIR/portuale-bin.<pid>` on every normal process exit -- a single site covering all seven call sites across three files, instead of per-command cleanup on every exit path. Fallback paths (vendored/checkout used directly) register nothing; signal kills still leak (same as any tmp dir). Measured: one phase-touching test process leaves 1 dir before, 0 after; `remove_bin_overlay_dir` idempotency pinned by unit test. The pmtest harness-side sweep stays as defense in depth. Original entry follows. **`bin_dir()`'s per-process PATH overlay is never removed (found auditing pmtest's own /tmp exhaustion, 2026-09-18).** `ebuild_phases.rs:666` makes a fresh `$TMPDIR/portuale-bin.<pid>` directory of symlinks (the `PORTAGE_BIN_PATH` overlay: vendored `bin/` over the `3rdparty/portage` checkout) on every process that runs real ebuild phases, and nothing ever deletes it -- not on success, not on error, not `atexit`. It hid inside `/tmp`'s general churn; pointing `$TMPDIR` at a dedicated directory (`../pmtest/USAGE.AGENTS.md` "Spazio /tmp") made it visible as ~500 tiny leftover directories per full contract-suite run, growing without bound run after run until pmtest started sweeping them itself from the *outside* -- a real leak in the product, not an artifact of the harness sweeping around it. Fix needs to run inside the same process (the harness can only clean up *after* the fact): either an RAII guard (`Drop` removing `overlay` when the last user is done) or an explicit `std::fs::remove_dir_all(&overlay)` on every exit path of the command that calls `bin_dir()` -- whichever fits the existing process lifetime better, since `bin_dir()` itself is a `OnceLock`, resolved once and read from many call sites for the rest of the process. Trivial in isolation; the only judgment call is where in the process's own shutdown path the removal belongs. Evidence: `../pmtest/pytests-contract-suite/conftest.py`'s `_reclaim_portuale_bin_overlays` (the harness-side sweep, commit `0a3397d` in pmtest) and its own commit message for the measured before/after counts.

The vendored `__dyn_clean` is untouched (byte-identical upstream). Verified: shell-level `rm -f` repro of both shapes, three `filesdir` unit tests, workspace + pretend suite green.

89. **DONE 2026-09-18 — `/etc/portage/package.*` directory fragments recurse with real's name filters.** `portage_util::recursive_config_files` ports `_recursive_file_list` exactly (arbitrary depth, one ascending sort per directory over files+dirs with in-place expansion, `VCS_DIRS` + dotfile + `~`-backup skips at every level including the root, symlinks followed), shared by `read_config_lines` (every `package.*`/`binrepos.conf`/profile read) and the `repos.conf` scan (which real also reads recursively); both halves landed together since recursion without filters would activate `.git/`+backups. Pins: `recursive_config_files_matches_reals_traversal_and_filters` unit test (mirrors the S0 `_recursive_file_list` oracle table incl. symlinks and root filtering). No existing fixture uses the directory form, so zero existing pins moved. Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #89. Original entry follows. **`/etc/portage/package.*` directory fragments are read one level deep, and real's name filters are missing (reported from the VM bed, 2026-09-18).** Real reads every one of these paths with `grabfile_package(..., recursive=1)` -> `portage/util/__init__.py::_recursive_file_list`, which walks a directory to **arbitrary depth**, skipping `VCS_DIRS` (`CVS RCS SCCS .bzr .git .hg .svn`, `const.py:278`), dot-prefixed names and `~`-suffixed backups (`_recursive_basename_filter`), and yielding files in **one merged ascending sort over files and directories, with each directory expanded in place** (stack DFS: `aa/inner` sorts between `00-first` and `nn-mid`; `zz/inner` before `zz-last`). Portuale's `portage-profile/src/lib.rs::read_config_lines` (~`:2044`) instead does a single `read_dir` filtered by `.filter(|p| p.is_file())` -- a subdirectory is silently dropped -- and applies **no** name filter, so it reads exactly the files real ignores. A second, independent copy of the same logic sits in `portage-repo/src/lib.rs` (~`:1143`) for `repos.conf`, which real also reads recursively (`repository/config.py:943-950`). Both halves are divergences and they point opposite ways, so **adding recursion without the filters makes things worse**: `.git/` and editor backups under `package.use/` would become live config. Reported from the VM bed as an `INVALID` run (`MERGE_RC=2`): the stock cloud image ships `/etc/portage/package.use/releng/no-filecaps` (`*/* -filecaps`), real honours it and merges the htop binpkg, portuale ignores the fragment, computes `filecaps` from the IUSE default, sees a binpkg USE mismatch and rebuilds from source; the same probe in the container bed (no `releng/` fragment) has both PMs agree, which is what isolates it. Reproduced host-side 2026-09-18 on a copied configroot: with `package.use/releng/frag` = `*/* nestedtestflag` and `package.use/zz-flat-control` = `*/* flattestflag`, real's `portage.config(config_root=...)['USE']` carries **both**, portuale's `emerge --info` carries only `flattestflag`; adding `zz-flat-control~` and `.hidden-frag` shows the mirror defect (real ignores both, portuale applies both). One `read_config_lines` feeds every `/etc/portage/package.{use,env,license,mask,unmask,accept_keywords,properties,accept_restrict}`, `binrepos.conf`, and the profile/repo-side `package.*`/`use.*`/`arch.list` reads, so the fix is two functions, not twenty call sites. No committed fixture uses the directory form at all today, which is why nothing caught it. Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) "#89". [C]

94. **DONE 2026-09-23 — declaration builtins assignment-expand an expanded name.** Shipped as Phase 6 S1–S4 (plan `01.94-brush-declaration-assignment.md`, batch `batch-2026-09-21.md`; fork branch `fix/declaration-assignment-expansion` S1 `dd016ba6` + S2 `38447d84`, fork `main` squash `eb3b6c7b`; portuale branch `backlog/94-brush-declaration-assignment`; re-pin `4edb1f43` → `eb3b6c7b`). S1 re-examines the expanded string inside the builtins (split on the first `=`, honour `+=`, compound `name=(…)` values untouched); S2 adds the compat matrix and unmarks 13 healed known-failures; S3 stages `docs/brush-pr/06-*`; S4 adds the `exportexpandpkg` fixture + `expanded_name_export_assigns_in_both_backends` guard and re-pins. Verified: brush-compat 2576 ran / 2120 succeeded / 0 unexpected, portuale workspace green, pmtest 1773 passed / 0 failed, live `ebuild --shell brush media-gfx/gimp-3.2.6 pretend` exits 0 past `pkg_pretend`. Original entry follows. `--shell brush` silently drops `export ${var}=value` (declaration builtins never assignment-expand an expanded name), killing `toolchain-funcs.eclass`'s `_tc-getPROG`; real `emerge --shell brush media-gfx/gimp` dies in `pkg_pretend` (filed 2026-09-20). Live: `portuale emerge --shell brush media-gfx/gimp-3.2.6` prints ` * Your current compiler does not support OpenMP!`, then `error: command not found: -E` twice, then `die "Active compiler does not have required support for OpenMP"`; the default `--shell bash` path is unaffected. Chain: `toolchain-funcs.eclass:25-45` `_tc-getPROG` ends with `export ${var}="${prog[*]}"; echo "${!var}"` (reached whenever `CC`/`BUILD_CC`/… is unset, i.e. the normal emerge env), and `tc-getCC` (`:54`/`:67`) therefore echoes **empty** under brush -- not an error, an empty string with status 0. `_tc-has-openmp` (`:647`) then runs ` -fopenmp test.c -o test` (the empty command expansion leaves `-fopenmp` as argv[0], `command not found`, stderr swallowed by its own `>&/dev/null`), returns nonzero, and `tc-check-openmp` (`:730`) `eerror`s + `die`s; its own diagnostics (`tc-is-gcc`/`tc-is-clang` -> `tc-get-compiler-type`, `:913-942`, `res=$($(tc-getCC "$@") -E -P - <<<"${code}")`) are where the two visible `command not found: -E` come from. Root cause is in brush, reproduced minimal on the pinned rev: `var=CC; prog=( gcc ); export ${var}="${prog[*]}"` leaves `CC=[]` (exit 0) under the embedded pin `b9524ad5` **and** under upstream `reubeno/brush` main `6bada559` (built and run 2026-09-20; still unfixed, no compat test covers it). brush's parser only recognizes an assignment word when the name is a *literal* identifier (`brush-parser/src/word.rs:1263` `assigned_scalar_name`), so `export ${var}=…` is a plain word; after expansion the builtin receives `CommandArg::String("CC=gcc")` and `export.rs:72-95`'s `String` branch only marks an *existing* variable exported -- a silent no-op (`declare`/`local` print `not a valid variable name`, `declare.rs:299`). bash instead performs the assignment after expansion for declaration builtins (`export`/`declare`/`local`/`readonly`/`typeset`; POSIX XCU 2.9.1), which is what every `_tc-getPROG` user relies on. Fix is upstream-first (sixth staged fix, e.g. `fix/declaration-assignment-expansion`: split `CommandArg::String` on the first `=` in the declaration builtins, or defer assignment detection for declaration commands), plus a brush compat case for the literal/`${var}`/quoted/append + `declare`/`local` forms and a Rust guard on the pin (an `export ${var}=`-using fixture, `heredocpkg`-style); this is a second, independent blocker to flipping the `--shell` default back to brush (alongside #5's PRs), and `docs/brush-pin.md` "What is *not* tracked here" records it. **Update 2026-09-20:** still unfixed on the 2026-09-20 re-pin (`4edb1f43`, upstream base `6bada559`); upstream's open, breaking [reubeno/brush#1280](https://github.com/reubeno/brush/pull/1280) ("centralize assignment expansion + overhaul declaration builtins") is the fix path — no sixth fork-staged fix was carried, and #5 now has four PRs to open, not five. **Update 2026-09-23:** shipped as Phase 6 S1–S4 (see the DONE line); the pin moved to `eb3b6c7b` carrying fix 06, and #5 is back to five PRs to open. [G]

95. **DONE 2026-09-20 (branch `backlog/95-package-env`: S0 `eb8bd23` + pmtest `30397dc`, S1 `f0a26bc`, S2 `0571aa3` + pmtest `64d4b9e`) — `package.env` vars now use real `_grab_pkg_env`'s acceptance set.** `match_package_env_vars` no longer narrows to `pretend::BUILD_VARS`: it rejects only real's `_non_user_variables` (dynamic `PROFILE_ONLY_VARIABLES` ∪ `env_blacklist` ∪ `CONFIG_PROTECT`), `PKGUSE`/`USE` (the latter on `Config::package_env_use`), and the keys portuale's own phase pipeline owns (`ENVIRON_FILTER`, `PORTUALE_COMPUTED`); empty values are kept (real blanks) and incrementals append within the container then fold onto the caller's base env with `-*`/`-tok` pruning and a sorted union (`regenerate()`). Pins: the accepted/rejected matrix, the three S0 incremental captures, scalar last-wins/empty-blanks (Rust units), the extended `penv-buildflags`/`penvbuildpkg` fixture pins, and the new hermetic `penvccpkg` `tc-getCC`/`tc-is-lto` end-to-end test with a stub compiler. Residues filed: #98 (`FEATURES`), #99 (`PORTAGE_TMPDIR`), #100 (standalone run-wide env), #101 (calling-env vs `pkg` layer precedence). Plan: [`history/01.95-package_env_acceptance.opus.md`](history/01.95-package_env_acceptance.opus.md); evidence: pmtest `differential-test-bed/findings/l2.md` "## #95 S0". Original entry follows. **`package.env` build vars are filtered to `pretend::BUILD_VARS`, dropping `CC`/`CXX`/`CPP`/`AR`/`NM`/`RANLIB`/`LD`/`RUSTFLAGS`/`CGO_*`/… — real merges `mail-client/thunderbird-156.0`, portuale dies in `pkg_pretend` -> `tc-is-lto` with `cc1: error: unrecognized argument to '-flto=' option: 'thin'` (found 2026-09-20, host `llvm-lto` env; not a brush bug — reproduced with the default `--shell bash`).** Host mapping: `/etc/portage/package.env/llvm-lto.conf:30` (`mail-client/thunderbird llvm-lto`) + `/etc/portage/env/llvm-lto` (`COMMON_FLAGS`/`CFLAGS`/`CXXFLAGS`/`FCFLAGS`/`FFLAGS` with `-flto=thin`, plus `CC=clang`, `CXX=clang++`, `CPP=clang-cpp`, `AR=llvm-ar`, `NM=llvm-nm`, `RANLIB=llvm-ranlib`, `LD`/`LDFLAGS=-fuse-ld=lld`, `RUSTFLAGS`, `CGO_*`; the host's other env files add `FEATURES` and `PORTAGE_TMPDIR`). `match_package_env_vars` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_phases.rs:1060`, shared by the merge path's `emerge_build::entry_package_env_vars` and the standalone path's `phase_standalone_base_env`) keeps only keys in `pretend::BUILD_VARS` (`rust/portuale/src/pretend.rs:6863`: `CFLAGS CXXFLAGS CPPFLAGS LDFLAGS FFLAGS FCFLAGS ASFLAGS MAKEOPTS CHOST CBUILD CTARGET`), so the same file's `CFLAGS=-flto=thin` lands but `CC=clang` is silently dropped; `tc-getCC` falls back to gcc and `tc-is-lto` (`toolchain-funcs.eclass:1268`, the gcc branch) fails. Evidence: real's resolved config for the atom (`portage.package.ebuild.config.config.setcpv`) is `CC=clang`, `CFLAGS=…-flto=thin…`; portuale alone reproduces it hermetically (`PORTAGE_TMPDIR=… portuale ebuild /var/db/repos/gentoo/mail-client/thunderbird/thunderbird-156.0.ebuild pretend` -> rc 1, identical trace, `temp/die.env` carries `CFLAGS='…-flto=thin…'` and **no `CC`**). **Real's acceptance model, verified in `3rdparty/portage`:** `_grab_pkg_env` (`lib/portage/package/ebuild/config.py:2269-2300`) copies **every** key of the env file into `configdict["pkg"]` except (a) `protected_keys` = the keys already in the pkg layer before package.env (`{PKGUSE}` at `setcpv`, `config.py:2029-2038`; `USE` is popped and re-appended around `package.use`, which therefore wins) and (b) `_non_user_variables` (`config.py:724-733` = the profile's dynamic `PROFILE_ONLY_VARIABLES` (`ARCH ELIBC KERNEL IUSE_IMPLICIT USE_EXPAND_*…`) ∪ `env_blacklist` (`_config/special_env_vars.py:15-81`: A AA BDEPEND CATEGORY DEPEND … SLOT SRC_URI …) ∪ `global_only_vars` (`CONFIG_PROTECT`)); a rejected key prints `!!! Illegal variable '<k>' assigned in '<file>'`; `INCREMENTALS` (`lib/portage/const.py:125`: `ACCEPT_KEYWORDS CONFIG_PROTECT_MASK ENV_UNSET FEATURES USE USE_EXPAND…`) **append** to the layer's current value instead of replacing; a `*/*` package.env entry goes to the global `conf` layer (`config.py:905-920`). So the legal per-package set is everything else: toolchain selectors (`CC CXX CPP AR NM RANLIB LD AS STRIP`), language/build flags (`RUSTFLAGS`, `GOFLAGS`, `CGO_*`, `NINJAOPTS`, `EMAKEOPTS`, `COMMON_FLAGS`, `LTOFLAGS`, `INSTALL_MASK`, `MAKEOPTS`, `DISTDIR`, `PORTAGE_TMPDIR`, `PORTAGE_NICENESS`, …), and the incrementals above. **Fix:** replace the `BUILD_VARS` filter with real's acceptance set — portuale already has the exclusion constants (`portage_profile::phase_environ::{ENVIRON_FILTER,ENV_BLACKLIST,PORTUALE_COMPUTED}`, `phase_environ.rs:64-240`; `ENV_BLACKLIST` is byte-for-byte real's `env_blacklist`) — keep `USE` on the existing `package_env_use` path, and give the incrementals append semantics. **Residues to file with the slice (narrower, deeper plumbing — currently resolved before/independently of package.env):** per-package `FEATURES` must reach `MergeOptions::features` and the `feature_enabled` gates (plus the phase env's folded `FEATURES`), and per-package `PORTAGE_TMPDIR` must feed the builddir path (the host's `tmpfs_on_disk` env is a live consumer). Pins: a fixture env file setting `CC` + representative selectors/incrementals, a `match_package_env_vars` unit test over the accepted/rejected sets (mirroring `_non_user_variables`), and an end-to-end `ebuild <fixture> pretend` in the `tc-getCC`/`tc-is-lto` shape with a stub compiler. [D]

96. **DONE 2026-09-20 — merge writes are atomic: `merge_tree` materializes the new file/symlink at a `.{name}._portage_merge_.{pid}` sibling and `rename(2)`s it over the destination (real `movefile`'s own same-device rename / copy-to-`#new`-then-rename), so the existing inode is never written to.** Shipped in `rust/portuale/src/ebuild_merge.rs`: `replace_file_atomic`/`replace_symlink_atomic`/`unique_sibling_path` (temp cleaned up on any failure), used by `merge_tree`'s obj and sym branches. Pins: `merge_tree_never_writes_the_existing_inode` (an open fd still reads the old bytes after the merge while the path carries the new content on a fresh inode — this is the mmap'd-library property, and it fails under the old `std::fs::copy`), `merge_tree_replaces_a_running_executable_atomically` (a live `/bin/sleep` copy survives the replacement, which the old code aborted with `ETXTBSY`), `merge_tree_replaces_a_symlink_without_temporaries`; workspace `cargo test --release` green. **Merge-path safety gate added** to `docs/agent-context.md` ("Merge-path safety gate") and `AGENTS.md` step 8: any merge-path change must be verified by a successful test merge of `sys-libs/glibc` and `app-shells/bash`. The bed cell is `differential-test-bed/atomlists/l1-merge-gate.txt` (pmtest) with `L1_CONSUME_REINSTALL=1` (`layers/l1/consume.sh`/`run/l1-merge-from-binpkg.sh`; forces `--reinstall-atoms`, records same-cpv reinstalls in the snapshot): live run `l1-20260920T141149Z` re-merged `sys-libs/glibc` + `app-shells/bash` over portuale's own running system — merge_rc 0, 4006 files compared, **0 hard / 0 unexplained**, only non-fatal mtime diffs. Original report follows. **`merge_tree` rewrites regular files in place (`std::fs::copy`), corrupting live mmap'd files: merging `sys-libs/readline` crashed every running `bash` (SIGSEGV in `_rl_forward_char_internal`), and the same path on `glibc` would take down the whole system (found 2026-09-20, host merge of `app-shells/bash`).** Live on this box: portuale merged `readline-8.3_p6` at 13:05:22 — `/usr/lib64/libreadline.so.8.3` `ctime=13:05:22` with `mtime=11:39:01` (the binpkg's own mtime copied onto the **existing** inode), no `PORT_LOGDIR` build log (real always logs; the p6 log does not exist, the `bash-5.3_p20` real-recovery log does), vdb entry written 13:05:22, and the preserved-libs registry still lists `readline-8.3_p3 -> /usr/lib64/libreadline.so.8.3` (every running bash had p3 mapped). First coredump 4 s later (13:05:26), then 13:06:13/38/52 and 13:08/13:09 — all `SIGSEGV`, all `_rl_forward_char_internal (libreadline.so.8 + 0x40616)` -> bash's fatal-signal re-raise (`si_code: SI_USER`) -> Konsole's `Program '/bin/bash' crashed.` The subsequent bash merge then aborted mid-`merge_tree` (all `/usr`+`/etc` files copied, `/bin/bash` not) and wrote no vdb entry, which is why real's recovery found the p20 doc/build-id files "unclaimed" with p15 still installed (`/var/log/portage/build/app-shells/bash-5.3_p20:20260920-110801.log.gz`). Root cause: `merge_tree`'s obj branch replaces a regular file with `std::fs::copy(&src, &write_dest)` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_merge.rs:1774`, binary merge via `merge_binpkg` `:3880`) — an `O_TRUNC` rewrite of the **existing inode**. Kernel asymmetry: `ETXTBSY` protects a running *executable* (the `/bin/bash` copy fails and aborts the merge), but an mmap'd *shared library* opens for write fine (verified live on this box), so the running processes' code pages are rewritten underneath their p3-resolved relocations and they segfault. Real `movefile` never touches the old inode: same-device `os.rename` (`3rdparty/portage/lib/portage/util/movefile.py:231`) or cross-device copy to `dest#new` then rename (`:346`). **`/etc/ld.so.cache` is eliminated** (portuale runs the same `<root>/sbin/ldconfig -X -r <root>` as real, `env_update.rs:206-226` vs `env_update.py:309`, after the merge; the loader reads the cache only at exec and it cannot segfault a running process). **Fix (this slice):** port real `movefile`: write the new content/owner/mode/mtime to a temporary sibling (`.{name}._portage_merge_.{pid}`, unique) and `rename(2)` it over the destination; same for symlinks (create temp link + rename, not remove+recreate). Regression test: a child process executing (and a second mapping) a file the merge replaces must survive the merge, the new content must land, the inode must change, and no temp sibling may remain. **Mandatory merge-path gate (added to `docs/agent-context.md` and `AGENTS.md` step 8): any change to the merge path must be verified by successful test merges of `sys-libs/glibc` and `app-shells/bash`** — the packages whose running files every process depends on — before the slice is considered done.

97. **DONE 2026-09-20 — `mrg`'s remote merge driver replaces files and links atomically (backlog #96 residue).** `MERGE_FLOW`'s obj branch now materializes the new file at `.${basename}._portage_merge_.$$` in the destination directory (`cp -p` + `chmod`/`chown --reference` applied to the temporary) and `mv -f`s it over the destination; the sym branch builds the link at the same temporary (`ln -s`, `touch -h -r`, `chown -h`) and renames it. Only a *symlink* destination is unlinked first, exactly like real `movefile`'s `os.unlink(dest)` for a symlink dest, so `mv` can never resolve a symlink-to-directory as a directory and the regular-file path never touches the live inode. The old `cp -p "$src" "$dest"` / `rm -f` + `ln -s` pair had the #96 failure mode on the client: `ETXTBSY` on a running executable, silent page rewrite for an mmap'd shared library. Pins (both execute the real generated driver locally through `run_merge_stage`): `merge_driver_never_writes_the_existing_inode` (an open fd still reads the old bytes after the merge while the path carries the new content on a fresh inode, no temporary left) and `merge_driver_replaces_a_running_executable_atomically` (a live `/bin/sleep` copy survives; the old code failed its `cp` with `ETXTBSY`). This is the driver-level half of the merge-path safety gate for `mrg` (a full client/server bed cell is not needed: the tests run the exact script the client executes); the local gate re-run after the change is `l1-20260920T145853Z` (glibc+bash re-merge, 0 hard / 0 unexplained). Original entry follows. **`mrg`'s remote merge driver still writes regular files with an in-place `cp -p` (backlog #96 residue).** The generated client shell in `rust/portuale/src/remote.rs`'s `MERGE_HELPERS` obj branch does `[ -L "$dest" ] && rm -f "$dest"; cp -p "$src" "$dest"` (`:2305`) and the sym branch `rm -f "$dest"; ln -s "$target" "$dest"` (`:2313-2314`) -- the exact non-atomic replacement #96 removed from `merge_tree`. On a client merging glibc/bash (or any package whose libraries/executables are live) this corrupts the running inode the same way: `ETXTBSY` on the running executable, silent page rewrite for mmap'd libraries. Fix: the same temp-sibling + `rename(2)` shape (the local `replace_file_atomic`/`replace_symlink_atomic` are `pub`-able for reuse or the shell gets an equivalent `mv` from a `$dest.merge.tmp.$$` sibling), plus the `mrg` variant of the merge-path safety gate (the remote driver's own glibc/bash client test). Not on the reported incident's path -- `mrg` is the opt-in remote driver -- hence filed rather than fixed with #96.

262. **DONE 2026-10-01 (batch-2026-09-29 Track M / M1: portuale `3ab54bdc`, merged `3b7b8490`; no pmtest half) — the leak is a staging-path bug, not an unscoped variable.** No portuale helper assigns an unscoped `x` in the env-save path (`bin/misc-functions.sh:388`, `bin/phase-functions.sh:798/1092/1177` all scope or `unset` it; the generated `mrg` scripts assign nothing). The `x=""` *value* is baked into the build-time `environment.bz2` shipped in the gpkg -- and the *leak* is that the regen never replaces it: `install_file_atomic`'s SSH arm staged the regen'd bytes into `TempDir::new("portuale-regen-install").keep()`, and since backlog #260 (`14456168`) `TempDir::new` **creates a directory**, so `std::fs::write` to that path fails `Is a directory (os error 21)` on every SSH install and the vdb keeps the build-time env verbatim (`!!! … merge-time environment regen failed at install, keeping build-time environment.bz2`). That is the whole intermittency: 09-26 leaked (pre-#171, no regen at all), 09-27/28 clean (regen + install worked), 09-29/30 leaked (#260 broke the staging). Filtering `x` would have masked the install failure and left the stale `buildpkg` FEATURES too, so the fix is at the source: stage into `server_dir.join("regen.env")` and clean up with `remove_dir_all`. TDD `remote::tests::install_file_atomic_ssh_stages_bytes_before_transport` watched RED (`Is a directory (os error 21)`, the exact bed error) and GREEN (the failure moves past staging to transport). Merge-path note in the commit: the client install is still `mv tmp dest` (rename(2), old inode never written). **Bed proof:** `l31-20261001T041932Z` and `l31-20261001T042840Z` both **0/0/0** -- the three `[VDB] porttest/{installmask,phases,setuid}-1.0/environment` rows are gone; `l32` 0/0/0 on 7 cells, unchanged; `l3-smoke` `l3-20261001T051513Z` hard 1 / explained 1 / 0 unexplained (glibc+bash re-merged from source, the SOURCE half of the gate); the consume-reinstall gate `l1-20261001T072430Z` rc 0, merged_count 2 (`app-shells/bash-5.3_p15`, `sys-libs/glibc-2.43-r2`), **0 hard / 0 explained / 0 unexplained**. *Original entry follows.* **OPEN (filed 2026-09-29, batch 09-28_244 Z0, `l31-20260929T152118Z` and re-run `l31-20260929T170017Z`) — the `mrg` merge path leaks a `declare -- x=""` global into the saved `environment`.** Both `l31` candidate runs of 2026-09-29 report 3 unexplained VDB rows: `porttest/{installmask,phases,setuid}-1.0/environment` carry one extra `declare -- x=""` line on the `mrg-remote` side, absent on the Portage side, which merges the same `PKGDIR`. The 09-26 runs (`l31-20260926T061121Z`, `…T061237Z`) had it too; the 09-27 and 09-28 candidates did not. It reproduced twice on 09-29, so it is not a one-off flake, and no merge-path code changed in between (`7f0fc8f3..feedb558`). Start from the phase that re-saves the environment on the remote side (some helper assigns an unscoped `x`); pin with an `l31` cell or a unit test on the saved env. [A]

78. **DONE 2026-09-18 (re-scoped, no product-code change) — `--backtrack=0` drops real's whole slot-op cascade. The shared S0 with #79 re-confirmed E0 verdict (b) on a staged installed matrix (provpkg 0/1 + five dep-key consumers in world): the dynamic-deps append gate only re-tags the provider and, with backtracking on, empties the plan where real rebuilds -- it is not the fix. The real gap is selection-time: portuale never uses the appended built-atom dep string as a satisfaction check during candidate selection (real's resting graph reads the consumer's recorded `provpkg:0/1=`, satisfied by the installed provider). That is #26-family work by the filing's own name, so #78 closes here and re-opens there -- no new pins, no behavior change. Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #78. Original entry follows. **`--backtrack=0` drops real's whole slot-op cascade; re-scoped by #78 E0 (verdict (b): the dynamic-deps append gate is not the fix).** Found by the #71 S0 control captures (host-exact, `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#71 S0"): `-uDvN --backtrack=0 --oneshot dev-libs/consrdep` (and `considep`) makes real print an **empty merge list**, rc 0, while portuale upgrades `provpkg` to 2.0. Real's `_slot_operator_trigger_reinstalls` is gated by `_allow_backtracking` (`depgraph.py:2131-2132`), cleared by `--backtrack=0` (`:12188-12190`), so with backtracking off neither the rebuilds nor the provider update happen; E0's real `--debug` confirms it (the bt0 cell has no `causing rebuilds` output, bt20 has it). **T13 correction:** the original entry claimed real's resting behaviour "relies on the built-atom dynamic-deps model (#26) which portuale does not model" -- portuale *does* model it (`installed_dep_string` + `built_slot_operator_atoms`, unit-tested) and gates it behind `PORTUALE_DYNAMIC_DEPS_APPEND`. E0's control experiment (host-exact slotop fixtures, both sides, portuale with and without the flag) gives **verdict (b)**: the flag does not reproduce real -- with `--backtrack=0` it only re-tags the provider (`r U`) and leaves the consumers unbuilt; with backtracking on it makes portuale print an **empty plan** where real rebuilds the three consumers and upgrades the provider -- and both #24 pins named in the gate's comment fail with it on (`slotchange-case4`'s libarchive `rR` and `slotundo-cascade`'s soccascb `rR` disappear). So the item is not the append gate; it needs the #26-family slot-op probe ordering (the gate's own doc comment already names it), re-scoped there rather than fixed by flipping the default. **Review refinement (2026-09-18, `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.4):** E0's "real's empty list is the backtracking gate, not a dynamic-deps model difference" is stronger than its evidence. `_allow_backtracking` explains why real schedules no *rebuilds*; it does not explain why real does not *upgrade the provider*, which portuale does in the same bt0 cell even with the append on (`r U`). Real's resting graph reads the consumer's recorded `provpkg:0/1=`, satisfied by the installed provider — an effect of the append portuale's append does not reproduce, because portuale applies it to the dep string but does not use it as a satisfaction check during candidate selection. That is the precise shape of the #26 work this item is re-scoped into. Evidence: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#71 S0" and "#78 E0"; plan `docs/history/02.75-79.md` Phase E/§7.

79. **DONE 2026-09-18 — a holdable plain pin from the reverse-dependency scan is ignored instead of enforced.** `reverse_dependency_constraints` now skips a *holdable* non-built-`:=` pin outright (real only ever consults such pins from the slot-operator update probe, `depgraph.py:2472-2538`): the D1 `rdcblocker-2.0` merge survives and its own `RDEPEND` blocker reports `[blocks B]` rc 1, byte-identical to the container oracle. Still enforced: built slot-operator pins (#24 rebuilds) and every not-holdable pin (dropped for the residual report -- keeper's shape). Pins: the D1 bed cell (both `portuale-bug` allowlist entries deleted), `test_reachable_installed_consumer_holdable_pin_does_not_block_an_upgrade` (rewritten: the old test pinned the over-enforcement; real upgrades cleanly, verified host-side incl. `--deep`). Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #79. Host-`@world` residue of the ignore filed as #91 (one stable extra upgrade, mechanism unidentified after 15 probes). Original entry follows. **A blocker-carrying candidate is dropped by the reverse-dependency pin scan instead of reported (the #74 S2b residue; corrected by #79 D0).** Host probe `-puDvN --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-containers/runc`: real upgrades `dev-build/gtk-doc-am` to `1.36.1`, whose own `!<dev-util/gtk-doc-1.36.1` soft block cannot be resolved (the installed `dev-util/gtk-doc-1.34.0-r2` is an `@selected` digraph node), prints `[blocks B]` and exits 1. Portuale instead silently settles on `gtk-doc-am-1.34.0` and exits 0. **D0's trace corrected the mechanism**: real never records a slot conflict at all (`--debug` has no `Slot conflict handler started.`), and portuale writes neither `MaskReason` variant nor the solvable `BacktrackFeedback::Config` -- the producer is `reverse_dependency_constraints` (the #54/#57 reverse-dependency scan), which enforces the installed `gtk-doc-1.34.0-r2`'s recorded `BDEPEND="~dev-build/gtk-doc-am-1.34.0"` as a `slot_constraints` atom once `gtk-doc-am` is upgrading; later passes filter `1.36.1` out of `matched` (`extra_constraints=["~dev-build/gtk-doc-am-1.34.0"]`). Real's `_slot_operator_check_reverse_dependencies` (`depgraph.py:2472-2538`) is only consulted from the slot-operator update probe and skips parents whose cp has another tracker instance (`:2513-2528`); portuale's scan runs for every upgrade of a version-pinned dependency, so a plain `~` BDEPEND pin from an installed consumer is enforced where real ignores it. **D1** reproduces it hermetically (`fixtures/` `rdcblocker`/`rdctarget`/`rdcpuller`, atomlist `l0-fixture-oracle-rdcpin.txt` run with `FX_WORLD_EXTRA=dev-libs/rdctarget`, two `owner: portuale-bug` allowlist entries). **D2 stopped per its own mandatory rule**: its two planned gates (a set-level `all_match` escalation gate in `collect_feedback`; real's `is_arg_parent` exclusion) do not match the identified producer, and the real fix belongs in the #54/#57 scan's parent set -- the machinery the rule protects. Re-scope: port real's actual `_slot_operator_check_reverse_dependencies` invocation gating (which parents the slot-operator probe visits) against the scan's current plain-pin enforcement, with the #57 `othermod`/`needer` and #54 residual cells as the regression guards. Evidence: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#74 S2b", "#79 D0"–"#79 D2"; plan `docs/history/02.75-79.md` Phase D.

80. **DONE 2026-09-18 — an unresolved row of a scan-collected blocker owner rides out as an orphan.** `resolve_blockers` now returns `FiledBlocker` (owner version attached); `file_blocker_conflicts` files entry-less *unsolvable* rows into `GraphResult::orphan_blockers` instead of dropping them, and the renderer prints them in the trailing blocker group, counts them in `Conflict: 1 block (1 unsatisfied)`, fires the `* Error` block and exits 1 -- A0 controls n1b/n6b byte-match real (host oracle, hermetic + test-local roots). `Replacement` rows can never orphan (they always carry `satisfied_by`). Original entry follows. **An unresolved row of a scan-collected blocker owner is dropped (#77 A1 residue).** Real's all-installed-packages scan (`_validate_blockers`, `depgraph.py:8919-9078`) also registers blockers of installed owners the run never walks; when such a blocker is unresolved (`unsolvable`), real prints `[blocks B]` in its trailing group and exits 1. Portuale's `file_blocker_conflicts` has no display home for an absent owner's unresolved row (only satisfied rows are tagged, and they ride their removal entry), so the row is dropped: A0 controls n1b (`=dev-libs/nomtarget-1.5` with the owner in the world file but no `@world` argument) and n6b (owner reachable through an installed world member) show real `B`/rc 1 vs portuale no row/rc 0. Fix: a trailing-orphan-row path (an orphan row list threaded into `package_counters_summary` and the trailing blocker group, or a display-only owner entry). Evidence: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#77 A0"/"#77 A1"; plan `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §6 named cut.

81. **DONE 2026-09-19 (branch `backlog/81-tree-scheduled-uninstall`, human-reviewed and merged as `fc07b88`) — tree-mode stuck-serializer row.** Tree mode's serializer schedules uninstalls flat mode never reaches; portuale showed no row (#75 C2 residue). Real's `_serialize_tasks` disables the greedy leaf pop in tree mode (`depgraph.py:9680`, `:9764-9777`), so the stuck-serializer branch (`:9998`) can schedule an uninstall and `:10351-10358` appends the solved blocker; C0's flat-vs-tree dumps show flat has no `Blocker` node and `scheduled_uninstalls: []` while tree has both, and real's tree retlist differs from its flat retlist (also reordering siblings: u6's `blockerpkg`, multislotparent's slots). Portuale's row predicate (`replacement_wait_index`) needs a dependency path from the replacement to the owner and cannot express the scheduled-uninstall state, so C1's placement port cannot show the row. Doing this needs `scheduled_uninstalls` modelled as serializer state (option (b) `docs/history/02.68-74.md` Phase D rejected as disproportionate), the same decision C2 stopped on. Evidence: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#75 C0"/"#75 C1"/"#75 C2"; plan `docs/history/02.75-79.md` Phase C; batch plan `docs/history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md` #81.

**S1+S2 status 2026-09-19 (branch `backlog/81-tree-scheduled-uninstall`, reviewed and accepted by the owner, merged to `main` as `fc07b88`; pmtest companion on `main`):** the tree-mode loop is ported, not a predicate patch -- `select_nodes` takes the real `not tree_mode` greedy gate plus the stuck branch (`scheduled_uninstalls` as synthetic leaf nodes, solved appended at selection), sharing digraph/bias/ladder/cycle code with the flat path; verdicts ride `BlockerConflict::tree_scheduled_uninstall` (resolve-side, gated on a pending Replacement row so the common case pays nothing) and feed only `--tree` Blocker nodes, the tree `-v` counters and nothing else (flat output, `--json`, exit codes untouched). Blocker nodes now sit right after their `after` entry in `node_order`, mirroring retlist adjacency (this fixed the prune eating the p2b row). Pins: `tree_simulation_solves_a_stalled_replacement_row` + `nvc_entries_do_not_participate_in_minimize_elimination` unit tests, `test_oracle_81_tree_only_satisfied_row_from_the_stuck_serializer` (row bytes + nesting + tree/flat counters + flat parity), CASES entry, default bed cell. Suite: 1724 passed (only the 3 pre-existing movepkg-dirt failures), Rust workspace green, clippy/fmt clean. **Residue (walk-sequence, NOT scheduler-verdict):** real leads the p2b tree with the merge owner and trails a nested new-slot owner occurrence, and nests the dep child as `[N]` where portuale nests `[U]`; u6 differs by the `blockerpkg-1.0` line position only (multislotparent is byte-identical). Those need display-walk sequencing surgery with every-tree-pin blast radius -- explicitly out of this branch; the row itself (the priced deliverable) is byte-exact with real byte-exact nesting and counters.

82. **DONE 2026-09-18 (branch `backlog/75-79-review-opus`) — `print_tree`'s edge selection is a drifted second copy of `build_digraph`'s, and the tree cross-mode invariant was weakened (#75 C1 review residue).** `DigraphPrelude::select_dep_target` now holds the narrowing once: `build_digraph` calls it with `merge_bound_only = false` (real's scheduler digraph edges to nomerge nodes) and the new public `resolved_dep_targets` with `true` for `print_tree` (portuale renders no line for an installed entry, so such an edge would open a hole in the tree) — the single documented difference, the shape #76 B1 established for the `||` selection; it also reports a suppressed `||` alternative as `None`, so `print_tree` dropped its own `kept_alt_branches` pass. Building the guard fixture exposed the larger half: `print_tree`'s `required_by` fallback for a deps-less entry is cp-keyed and `required_by` lists every owner that pulled *any* slot of the cp, so a slot an owner never asked for gained that owner as a tree parent; the fallback now skips an owner whose own atom already resolved to another instance. Container oracle (`TEST/findings/l0.md` "#82"): `--tree dev-libs/slotorderroot` is byte-identical to real 3.0.82.2 for the first time (the slice's one corpus drift, reviewed against that capture and blessed), pinned by `test_oracle_82_tree_edges_follow_the_resolved_dep_target`; new fixtures `dev-libs/treeslotpkg`/`treeslotparent`/`treeslotuser` + two `merge_order` unit tests pin the vercmp tie-break and the `merge_bound_only` split. The invariant checker's `--tree` row dedupe is gone (dead: `rows_key` already drops every `[nomerge]` row) with `test_checker_flags_a_merge_row_printed_twice_in_tree_mode` as the guard; the bidirectional `hidden_path_to` is **kept** — its reason is the unparsed `[blocks …]`/`[uninstall …]` rows collapsing the parsed depth (B0b's p2c), not the `[nomerge]` rows, and the comment now says so. Bed 28 probes / 22 clean / 18 explained / 0 unexplained. Residue: backlog **#85** (the two new slots merge in the opposite order, so the `treeslotuser` tree cannot be byte-pinned). Original entry follows. **`print_tree`'s edge selection is a drifted second copy of `build_digraph`'s, and the tree cross-mode invariant was weakened (#75 C1 review residue).** Two small, independent defects found reviewing the `backlog/75-79` merge (`docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.1/§7.2). (a) `pretend.rs::print_tree` re-implements `build_digraph`'s dependency-edge narrowing instead of reusing it, and ranks the tie with a **string** compare (`jv.cmp(bv) == Ordering::Greater`, `pretend.rs:1853`) where `merge_order.rs:1772` uses `portage_versions::vercmp` — so `1.9` outranks `1.10` and slot `9` outranks slot `10`, and any atom matching two merge-bound entries of the same `cp` can nest under the wrong instance in tree mode; no pin catches it because the only committed multi-slot shape is `1.0`/`2.0`, where the two comparisons agree. Its `merge_bound` tie-break arms are also dead code (the candidate loop already skips non-merge-bound entries). Fix: export `build_digraph`'s narrowing as one shared helper, the way `#76 B1` did for `suppressed_alt_edges` — that precedent is exactly why the `||` selection cannot drift today — or at minimum swap in `vercmp` and delete the dead arms, with a multi-slot `9`/`10` fixture as the guard. (b) `tests/output_invariants.py::check_cross_mode`'s `if mode == "--tree": got = sorted(set(got))` is unnecessary: `rows_key` already filters `kind != "nomerge"`, so the ancestor occurrence never reaches `got`; removing the two lines leaves `tests/test_output_invariants.py` + `tests/test_emerge_pretend_contract.py` green (1222 passed, 5 xfailed), and keeping it hides exactly the duplicate-row bug the invariant exists to catch. The same function's `hidden_path_to(a,b) or hidden_path_to(b,a)` relaxation *is* needed, but should be narrowed to the `[nomerge]` ancestor rows that motivate it. Evidence: `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.

83. **DONE 2026-09-18 (branch `backlog/75-79-review-merged`) — the all-installed blocker scan makes a narrow `emerge -p` slower, and re-runs on every backtracking pass (#77 A1 review residue).** Profiled first (`TEST/findings/l0.md` "#83"): on `-p --oneshot app-editors/nano`, where the walk reaches four packages and the scan visits 2084, the cost is two halves — 391 ms in `list_candidates` locating each package's live ebuild metadata and 435 ms in `use_reduce_flat_disjunctive` (57 ms in the vdb reads, 0.5 ms listing the vdb). The reduce is now skipped when the dep string has no `!`: a blocker token always begins with one (`portage_dep::Blocker`) and `use_reduce` drops and expands tokens but never rewrites them, so the filter is exact; 1804 of the 2084 packages on this host are skipped. **1.22–1.30 s → 0.99–1.01 s** on `nano` (real 3.0.82.2: 2.23–2.61 s for the same argv), 13.86 → 13.3 s on `-puDvN --oneshot app-containers/runc`, ~57 → ~54 s on `@world`. Two cheap guards from the parallel review join it: the scan returns before touching the vdb when **no** entry is merge-bound (every row it could produce is `owner_installed`, so `resolve_blockers` would take the `!owner_merging && installed_match && !merge_bound_match` early `continue` on all of them -- an exact shortcut, not an approximation), and the walked-cp test is a `HashSet` instead of a linear `entries` scan per installed package (O(installed x entries) -> O(installed)). Neither moves this host's numbers (both shapes measured here have merge-bound entries and are I/O-bound), so they are complexity removal, not a measured win. A third variant of the `!` prefilter was assessed and **rejected**: reading the raw vdb `IDEPEND`/`PDEPEND`/`RDEPEND` *before* the live tree lookup would skip `list_candidates` too, but real reads these through the `FakeVartree` dbapi (`depgraph.py:9016` `vardb.aux_get`), whose `_apply_dynamic_deps` substitutes the **live ebuild's** deps under the default `--dynamic-deps=y` (`FakeVartree.py:142-146`) -- so an ebuild revised after the install to add a blocker is a blocker real sees and that prefilter would not. The cost it targets is #86's, and #86 removes it without the fidelity loss. Two further mitigations were measured and deliberately not taken: hoisting `PassState::installed_meta_memo` into `ResolveCtx` buys ~57 ms per extra pass (~170 ms on the four-pass `runc` probe) for a `RefCell` borrow across four call sites, and the residual `list_candidates` cost is the cold fill of its own process-global cache, free from the second pass on. The filing's "~4x" headline is corrected by the numbers: the scan is work real does too, and portuale answers the same narrow query in under half real's time. Residue: backlog **#86** (follow real's `myrepo=pkg.repo` instead of searching the repos). Original entry follows. **The all-installed blocker scan makes a narrow `emerge -p` ~4x slower, and re-runs on every backtracking pass (#77 A1 review residue).** `#77 A1`'s cost note ("56.2 s -> 58.3 s") measured the one shape where `collect_unwalked_installed_blockers` is nearly free: a complete-mode `@world` walk already has an entry for almost every installed package, so the scan's skip filter fires at once (measured 4.4–5.0 ms/pass). Instrumented on this host at `f818d1d`: `-p --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-editors/nano` goes **0.32 s -> 1.26 s** (854 ms of scan in a single pass), `-puDvN --oneshot app-containers/runc` 12.65 s -> 14.15 s (282–290 ms x 4 passes), `sys-fs/udisks` 9.63 s -> 11.01 s. The per-pass repetition is structural: `run_pass` runs once per `Backtracker` iteration (up to `--backtrack`, default 20) and `PassState::installed_meta_memo` is per-pass. Three behaviour-preserving mitigations, in order: (1) hoist the metadata memo into `ResolveCtx` so passes 2..N reuse it; (2) `if !depstr.contains('!') { continue; }` before the token split — a blocker token always starts with `!` and `use_reduce` never introduces one, so it is exact; prototyped here it cuts the recurring pass 282 ms -> 149 ms and `nano`'s first pass 854 ms -> 676 ms (280 of 2088 installed packages carry a `!` in `IDEPEND PDEPEND RDEPEND` at all); (3) if that is not enough, cache the scan's blocker-token output per installed instance for the process lifetime — the only entries-dependent part is `disjunction_preference`, which matters solely for a blocker inside a `|| ( … )` group. Evidence: `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.3.

84. **DONE 2026-09-18 (branch `backlog/75-79-review-merged`) — the kept-`|| ( … )`-branch set was re-derived once per blocker row (`#76 B2` review residue).** `replacement_wait_index` (`pretend.rs`) called `portage_repo::kept_alt_branches` on every invocation, and `blocker_row_disposition` invokes it once per `Replacement` row from four walks (`collect_inline_blocker_lines`, `trailing_blocker_lines`, `count_blocker_rows`, the `--tree` node walk), so one row paid the O(graph) derivation four times over. The derivation itself was never wrong — it shares `suppressed_alt_edges` with `build_digraph`, so the #53 self-branch exclusion cannot drift — only its placement. `kept_alt_for_display` now derives it once per **walk** and threads `&[HashSet<usize>]` down into `replacement_wait_index`, which reads a missing entry as "suppressed" through `get` (so an empty set cannot panic); the `print_entry_line` call site passes `&[]` outright, since only `unsolvable` rows reach it and `resolve_blockers` writes `satisfied_by: None` whenever `unsolvable`. **The hoist is per walk, not per list:** `trailing_blocker_lines` is called once per display entry from `print_entry_line`, so deriving at list level instead paid it once per entry — measured here as a 12.4 s -> 15.8 s regression on `emerge -p @world` before the gate was narrowed to the rows each walk actually dispositions. With that gate the cost is at most four derivations per run and zero when no `Replacement` row exists, against the old once-per-row-per-walk; no measurable change on this host's shapes (`-p @world` 12.3 s, `-p --oneshot app-editors/nano` 0.99-1.00 s, `-puDvN @world` 53.5-54.0 s, all within noise of the pre-slice build), so this is an algorithmic ceiling removed, not a measured win. Pin: `kept_alt_for_display_is_derived_once_and_shared` (the shared set equals the derivation it replaces; the owner-restricted form derives only for the owner carrying the row; the kept branch waits and the empty set hides). Evidence: this commit; plan `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.6.

85. **DONE 2026-09-18 — S0 proved this is not the #17 wall: real's `_minimize_children`.** Real's `--debug` digraph dump shows `treeslotuser` has NO edge to 1.10: `_minimize_children` (`depgraph.py:4751-4856`) eliminates the redundant 1.10 selection (`:0` matches only 1.9, the bare atom matches both), so the bias ties 1-1 and discovery order decides. Ported into the shared `select_dep_target` (both `build_digraph` and `print_tree` via `resolved_dep_targets`); NVC entries are excluded from elimination (they are never selected packages -- real yields `(atom, None)`), preserving the `opartlya` disclosure order the elimination otherwise flips. The bed cell is byte-equal flat and tree; the `treeslot-new-slot-merge-order` allowlist entry is deleted. Pins: two `merge_order` unit tests + `test_oracle_85_unqualified_atom_collapses_onto_the_slot_qualified_target` (flat `-v` and `--tree` byte pins). Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #85. Original entry follows. **Two new slots of one cp, pulled by different parents, merge in the opposite order (#82 residue).** Found while building #82's tree fixture, and the reason that fixture gets no byte pin. `dev-libs/treeslotuser` pulls `dev-libs/treeslotpkg:0` (`1.9`), `dev-libs/treeslotparent` (which pulls `:1`, `1.10`) and a slot-unqualified `dev-libs/treeslotpkg`; real 3.0.82.2 merges `1.9, 1.10, treeslotparent, treeslotuser` while portuale's `build_digraph` discovery order reaches the slot-1 node first and emits `1.10, 1.9`. Set, versions, flags and `Total:` all match — only the sequence differs, the standing #17 merge-order class. It propagates into `--tree`, whose display list is the reversed merge order: after #82 the tree *edge set* is real's (`treeslotparent`'s only child is `1.10`), but the nesting still differs because the walk meets the slots in the other order. Capture: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#82", bed cell `-v dev-libs/treeslotuser` with the `treeslot-new-slot-merge-order` allowlist entry (`owner: portuale-bug`), which is deleted when this closes.

86. **DONE 2026-09-18 — installed-package live metadata comes from the vdb-recorded repository.** `live_metadata_for_installed` threads the recorded `repository` through all four `installed_dep_string` call sites (scan, resolver loop, `--deep` recursion, merge-order closure) as one shared helper, replacing `list_candidates` priority search; missing/removed recorded repo falls back to the old search (minimal fixture vdbs predate the file), and a version gone from the recorded repo falls back to Raw. Pins: `live_metadata_for_installed_prefers_the_recorded_repo` unit test + `tworepoowner`/`tworepotarget` two-repo fixtures + `test_oracle_86_installed_metadata_comes_from_the_recorded_repo` (old binary printed a spurious uninstall+b pair; fixed binary and real print the clean upgrade). Timing re-measured 2026-09-18 on the #83 probe shape (`-p --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-editors/nano`, this host, interleaved warm runs): 0.99-1.01 s -> **0.83-0.85 s** (cold first run 4.09 s), against real 3.0.82.2's 2.23-2.61 s -- a real win, below the 0.60 s upper bound exactly as predicted (the faithful single-file read keeps more than the unfaithful skip). Order-bed re-runs pending (no container image in this environment; see the close-out). Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #86. Original entry follows. **The live ebuild metadata of an installed package is found by searching the repos, not by its recorded repository (#83 residue).** Real's `FakeVartree._aux_get_wrapper` passes `myrepo=pkg.repo` — the vdb-recorded `repository` — straight to `portdb.aux_get` (`FakeVartree.py:136`), so the dynamic-deps view of an installed package always comes from the repo it was built from. Portuale resolves it with `list_candidates(repos, cat, pkg)` + `max_by_key(repo_priority)` at every `installed_dep_string` call site (`collect_unwalked_installed_blockers`, `enqueue_dependencies`, the `--deep` display-list recursion), which differs whenever the same version exists in two repos and the recorded one is not the highest-priority. It is also the single biggest remaining cost of the #83 scan: a `read_dir` per installed package (391 ms for 2084 on this host) where the recorded repository would need one file read (~5 ms total). The prize is measured, not estimated: a third review branch (`backlog/r01oc`) skipped the per-package lookup outright — by the unfaithful raw-record route this item exists to replace — and `emerge -p --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-editors/nano` went **0.99-1.00 s -> 0.60 s** on this host, interleaved, three runs each. That is the win available here faithfully. Fidelity change, so it needs its own oracle — a two-repo fixture where the same cpv exists in both, with the vdb `repository` naming the lower-priority one — and all the call sites must move together. Evidence: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#83".

Deliberately left out of #109 S3 (`08.109-110-vdb-metadata-snapshot.md` §5, §6). **2026-09-23 update:** #126 (Phase 6b S2, O5 ruling) ports the *empty/missing*-`SLOT` half — `read_installed_slot` returns `Some("0")` through the seam. 167. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G2 S1 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, run `l32-20260926T073144Z`; review fix round 1, 2026-09-26) — portuale writes `/var/lib/portage/preserved_libs_registry` unconditionally, with real-divergent JSON, and with the wrong owner cpv / path order.** Real `PreservedLibsRegistry.store()` is `json.dumps(..., indent="\t", sort_keys=True)` and rewrites the file only when the registry changes: with no preserved-lib activity the packaged empty file stays **0 bytes** (C1 `-C`/`--depclean`, C3 `CONFIG_PROTECT`, C4 `--resume`), and an empty registry real *does* write is exactly `{}` (**2 bytes**, no trailing newline; C2 soname bump). Portuale's hand-rolled `ebuild_merge::write_plib_registry` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_merge.rs:1116`) is called unconditionally from `register_preserved_libs` (`:1414`), `unregister_preserved_libs` (`:1203`) and `prune_unused_preserved_libs` (`:1594`), so every merge/unmerge rewrites the file even when nothing is preserved, and it emits `"{\n"` … `"}\n"`, so an empty registry is `{\n}\n` (**4 bytes**) and a non-empty one carries a trailing newline real omits. The comparator reports `SIZE /var/lib/portage/preserved_libs_registry` in all four cells (`0 vs 4` in C1/C3/C4, `2 vs 4` in C2). **The C2 *content* is the second half of the same class, and it was triaged in review fix round 1 (it is the exact shape the C2 cell exists to check).** After the soname bump, portuale's `preserved_libs_registry-after.txt` records the owner cpv `l32/sonamelib-1.0` (the pre-bump cpv, which no longer owns the files) and the path order `[…/libl32soname.so.1, …/libl32soname.so.1.0.0]`, while real records owner `l32/sonamelib-2.0` and the **reversed** order `[…/libl32soname.so.1.0.0, …/libl32soname.so.1]` — exactly the S0 oracle's blessed keys (`findings/l5.md` `## Group 2`: "the owner cpv `l32/sonamelib-2.0`; the path list order (`.1.0.0` then `.1`)"). The comparator surfaces only the size row for this file (the size difference dominates); the content divergence was found by inspecting both `preserved_libs_registry-after.txt` files. Fix: write only on an actual registry change, serialize empty as `{}` (and drop the trailing newline), assign the registry entry to the **new** (post-bump) cpv, and order the preserved path list `…so.1.0.0` before `…so.1` — the same registry and the same `write_plib_registry`/`register_preserved_libs` call family, hence one item, not a sibling. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l5.md` "## #32 S1" class A, and `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l32-20260926T073144Z/C2/candidate/{portuale,portage}.logs/preserved_libs_registry-after.txt`. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `2d2246ec`; gate `l1-20260926T224350Z` green and `l32-20260926T230337Z` shows the SIZE row gone C1–C4+F2 with C2 content equal. [I]

169. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G1 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, candidate run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) — remote merge drops `setuid`/`setgid` (and `sticky` for a non-root client); the receive-stage `tar -xf` is only one of the two sites.** The bed's candidate pair (`mrg --remote-binpkg` over a loopback sshd into the container ROOT; run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) reports `[MODE] /usr/bin/pt-setuid` `4711` → `711` and `[MODE] /usr/bin/pt-setgid` `2755` → `755`; `pt-sticky` survives `1750`. Re-deriving S0's boundary overturned its single-site localization. **(a) Root client:** the receive `tar -xf "$BUNDLE"` preserves all three bits, and the loss is the merge copy driver's order — `MERGE_FLOW`'s obj branch does `chmod --reference "$src" "$tmp"` **then** `chown --reference "$src" "$tmp"` (`rust/portuale/src/remote.rs`), and `chown(2)` clears `S_ISUID`/`S_ISGID` on a regular file even when the owner is unchanged (the sticky bit is not cleared): final `0711`/`0755`/`1750`. **(b) Non-root client** (S0's `uid 1000` host capture): the receive `tar -xf` **without `-p`** strips all three at extraction (`4711/2755/1750` → `711/755/750`; `tar -xp` keeps them), and the copy stage never sees them. Both sites are product bugs: add `-p` to `unpack_script`'s `tar -xf`, and re-chmod (or chown before chmod) in the obj/sym branches. Read-only reproduction in the bed image: `cp -p` alone and `cp -p` + `chmod --reference` keep `4711/2755/1750`; adding `chown --reference` yields `711/755/1750`; `chown` then `chmod` keeps all three. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l4.md` "## #31 S2"; `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l31-20260926T061556Z/candidate/mrg.files.tsv` (`pt-setuid 711`, `pt-sticky 1750`). **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track L): portuale `171f2abb`; gate `l1-20260926T223731Z` green and `l31-20260926T225952Z` shows the `[MODE]` rows gone. Tried to use non-existent binary for 'l32/faultpkg-1.0'` → `!!! Likely caused by an outdated index. Run 'emaint binhost -f'.`; (b) with a completely empty `PKGDIR` it **falls back to the ebuild** (`>>> Emerging (1 of 1) l32/faultpkg-1.0::l32`), builds from source, rc 0, and installs `/usr/share/l32/faultpkg` (+ the vdb entry). Portuale's `emerge_getbinpkg::refresh_binhost_indexes` (`rust/portuale/src/emerge_getbinpkg.rs:63-104`) shells `wget` directly, so the log is the raw wget transcript (`--...-- http://127.0.0.1:18765/Packages.gz` … `500 Internal Server Error`), and its caller `pretend.rs:11395-11401` turns any refresh error into `emerge: binhost ...: wget failed to fetch "http://127.0.0.1:18765/Packages" (exit status: 8)` + `ExitCode::from(1)` **before resolution**, so both (a) and (b) abort and nothing is installed (comparator row `MISSING /usr/share/l32/faultpkg`, F3b). Fix direction: emit real's `!!! [<repo>] Error fetching binhost package info` / `!!! HTTP Error 500` message shape, treat a failed remote index refresh as non-fatal, and let the resolver fall back to the ebuild when no usable local binary exists. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l32-20260926T083914Z/F3/candidate/{portage,portuale}.logs/{binhost-merge.log,binhost-fallback.log,binhost-state.txt,binhost-fallback-state.txt}`; `findings/l5.md` "## #32 S2" class E. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `de555da2` + pmtest `a278174`; gate `l1-20260927T065535Z` green and l32 F3 `l32-20260927T070047Z` is 0 unexplained. [I]

176. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-C2a #158 S1 scope fix) — the `info/dir` OWNER direction is a distinct mechanism: port real's post-emerge GNU-info regeneration.** `/usr/share/info/dir` is in no package's `CONTENTS` — portage deletes it from every image (`bin/misc-functions.sh:141-145`, "Portage regenerates this on the installed system") and rebuilds it once at the end of an emerge when `noinfo` is not in `FEATURES` (`util/_info_files.py::chk_updated_info_files`, called from `_emerge/post_emerge.py:128-129`); `install-info` writes the new `dir` as the running process's own uid/gid (root, hence real `0:0`). Portuale has no equivalent (its merge log has no `Regenerating GNU info directory index...` line), so the container base's file with its `1:1` owner survives untouched — the L3-core OWNER row real `0:0` vs portuale `1:1`, same sha/mode/size (`f4d573…`, 644, 19332). This is a feature (install-info spawn + dir-mtime memo + output), not the #158 S1 `REPLACING_VERSIONS`/`movefile` leave-alone mechanism that fixed the opposite `awk.1` direction, so `#158` S1's scope fix split it out rather than folding it in. Smallest repro: any real emerge with an info file and `/usr/bin/install-info` present (`dev-lang/python` ships several). Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l3.md` "## #158 S0". **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `42bf7afe` + pmtest `02918af`; gate `l1-20260926T225636Z` green and the l3-core `info/dir` OWNER row closed via P-C3. [I]

Shape: a binary package (`--usepkg`) whose recorded `:=` dep is bound to an abandoned provider sub-slot, with a same-slot ebuild that accepts the visible provider. [A]

266. **DONE 2026-10-02 (batch-2026-09-29 Track R / R7, merged `31953983`) — `minimize_children` reads the candidate's effective USE before collapsing. Original entry follows.** **OPEN (filed 2026-09-29, #236 Slice B / #107 D2) — `_minimize_children` never matches a use-dep atom against a not-yet-built package.** Real's elimination matches every selected package with `findAtomForPackage(pkg, modified_use=self._pkg_use_enabled(pkg))` (`_emerge/depgraph.py:4797-4807`); portuale's `minimize_children` does that for installed packages (vdb USE/IUSE, #107 D2) but counts a use-dep atom as matching only its own pick for an ebuild/binary candidate, so a collapse that needs the candidate's effective USE (e.g. `>=foo-1[bar]` alongside `<foo-3` on an ebuild parent, both satisfiable by the lower pick) keeps both selections where real drops one. Conservative (it can only keep a package real drops, never the reverse), and no L0 probe or fixture shows it; fix by evaluating the candidate's effective USE the way the walk's own selection does. [A]

## Tier 4 — `--solver=` real-tree correctness (pubgrub / resolvo)

33. **`--solver=resolvo` cycle linearization** — `install_order` can't order any closure with a toolchain cycle (glibc↔gcc↔perl); `solver_bridge.rs` prints raw ids. Non-functional on real targets. `docs/history/solver-backends-analysis.md`. [J]
34. **`--solver=pubgrub` over-merge** — feeds pubgrub the over-approximated `flag?()` reachability closure as the real graph (`nodejs`: 48 vs 8). Feed the resolved graph instead. `solver_bridge.rs`. [J]
